### Image prompt

```text
@Image 1 is a real photograph shot on an iPhone from inside a crowd of neighbours on a driveway, looking up at a man on a house roof. Reproduce it EXACTLY — the same house, the same grey shingle roof, the same stone-and-siding facade with the house number plaque, the same dark garage door, the same aluminium extension ladder running steeply up through the middle of the frame, the same man in the dark jacket and orange beanie standing on the roof, the same crowd of neighbours seen from behind filling the bottom third and cropped by both side edges with nobody facing the camera, the same overcast winter daylight, the same colours.

CHANGE EXACTLY TWO THINGS:

1. MORE CLEAN SKY AT THE TOP. Tilt the camera up very slightly and pull back a touch so that the TOP FIFTH of the vertical frame is completely empty flat overcast white-grey sky — no roof, no ladder, no people, no wires, nothing in that band. The roofline, the man and the inflatable all sit BELOW that empty band. The rest of the composition stays the same.

2. THE THING ON THE ROOF MUST CLEARLY READ AS A SHOP-BOUGHT AIR-BLOWN INFLATABLE SANTA CLAUS, WITH DUCT TAPE ON IT. It is a large half-inflated nylon yard Santa, sagging and slumped over on the shingles beside the man: an unmistakable bright RED coat and red trousers with WHITE fluffy fur trim at the cuffs, hem and hood, a WIDE BLACK BELT with a gold buckle across the belly, a big round WHITE BEARD and a pale printed face, a red pointed hat with a white pom-pom flopping sideways, and a round pot belly that is partly deflated so the nylon wrinkles and folds. It is obviously a cheap printed nylon inflatable, not a person and not a red blanket. Wrapped across it are several WIDE STRIPS OF SILVER DUCT TAPE — one long strip taped across its chest and over the shingles, another around its arm, another across its leg, plainly holding it down to the roof. The tape is clearly visible silver-grey and slightly crumpled. The man is leaning over it with one hand on the Santa as if pressing another strip of tape down. It must be large and readable — roughly half the frame width, in the upper-middle of the frame.

Everything else is identical to @Image 1. Do NOT change the crowd, do NOT turn any faces towards the camera, do NOT move the camera further back from the house, do NOT shrink the man.

Hyperrealistic amateur iPhone photograph, extremely high quality, perfect natural overcast daylight, real nylon and shingle texture, natural contact shadows, slight handheld imperfection. Not a render, not CGI, no text, no watermark, no logo.
```

### Video prompt

```text
The attached start frame is frame one. Animate it exactly as it is. Real documentary footage shot on a phone by a bystander in the crowd.

CAMERA: locked handheld. No zoom in, no zoom out, no push, no pull, no dolly, no pan, no tilt, no orbit, no crane, no rack focus, no reframe. The framing at 5.0s is identical to the framing at 0.0s. Only tiny involuntary handheld micro-jitter of one or two pixels. NOTHING of the person filming is visible at any moment - no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no device, nothing along the bottom edge.

TIMELINE, real time, everything obeys real gravity and real weight:
0.0-1.0s  The man is fighting with a roll of packing tape, trying to strap the sagging inflatable Christmas decoration down. He rips a length off, it curls and sticks to itself, he slaps it on and it peels straight back off. His jaw is clenched, his face is red, he is furious.
1.0-1.8s  He yanks on it with both hands, leaning his whole bodyweight back.
1.8-2.8s  His foot slips out from under him. He goes down hard and fast - real bodyweight, no hang time, no float - and at the same instant the inflatable tears free and topples DOWN THE SAME SIDE HE IS ON, the near side facing the camera. It deflates as it goes, the nylon wrinkling and sagging, and it slides down and lands on the ground right next to him, in the lower half of the frame, within arm's reach of his body. CRITICAL: the inflatable NEVER rises, NEVER floats, NEVER lifts into the sky, NEVER goes over to the far side away from him, NEVER leaves ground level. It falls straight down on his side and stays on the ground.
2.8-3.6s  He lands heavily, hip and shoulder first, dust and dry grass puffing up. The inflatable settles into a crumpled flat heap beside him, still twitching as the last air escapes.
3.6-5.0s  The neighbours in the foreground freeze - shoulders jolt, heads snap toward him, two of them flinch backwards, one raises a hand to their head. They stay seen FROM BEHIND for the whole clip; no face turns toward the camera at any point.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only - wind, tape ripping, a heavy thud of a body on ground, nylon collapsing, a grunt, and shocked gasps from the crowd: "Oh my God!" "Whoa!" No music, no score, no background song, no sound design.

LOOK - hyper realistic, photographic, indistinguishable from a real phone clip, bright midday sun: harsh real daylight with a slightly blown-out sky and crushed shadows, natural skin texture with pores and shine, hair moving in the wind, real cloth wrinkles, light motion blur on fast-moving limbs and fabric, faint rolling-shutter wobble, mild sensor noise in the shadows, one small autofocus hunt, slightly imperfect exposure. Comedic, nobody is hurt.
BANNED: animation, cartoon, illustration, stylised or synthetic imagery, plastic or waxy skin, weightless or floating motion, anything rising into the sky, rubbery limbs, objects sliding without friction, smooth interpolated motion, slow motion of any kind, glossy over-clean lighting, morphing faces or hands, extra fingers, warping straight lines, text, captions, watermark, logo.
```

### Rejected takes

- **v27_c01_g1.png · take 1** — “Camera and the people must be closer to the house because the man on the roof it’s almost invisible.”
- **v27_c01_g2.png · take 2** — “I need a little space at the top so the text i will add in capcut will be visible, and also it should be more cleared that is a santa inflatable christmas decoration and I want tape on it.”
- **v27_c01_g3.png · take 3** — Superseded by g4, which is a chained edit off this frame. No reason recorded on this line.
- **v27_c01_g1.mp4 · take 1** — “it’s looking very fake, The inflatable christmas decoration must fall in the same side as him not in the sky, it’s just not making sense”

### What made it work

A crowd is safest from behind. About twenty neighbours, specified as rear and three-quarter-rear views in loose clusters with dogs, a phone filming and parked cars. Rear views were chosen deliberately to avoid twenty AI faces, and the crowd stays a silhouette bank that reads as a real bystander video.

Name the parts, not the object. “Make it clearly a Santa inflatable” does not work. Naming the fur trim, the belt buckle, the beard, the pom-pom and the wrinkled nylon is what makes it read as an inflatable Santa rather than a red shape.

g1 was passed a frame for SUBJECT AND ACTION ONLY. v23_c01_g1.png supplied the man, the faded inflatable and the ladder — with its camera explicitly excluded, because v27 needed a completely different wide angle from across the street.

### Video prompt

```text
TWO SCENES IN ONE CLIP, JOINED BY ONE HARD CUT AT EXACTLY 2.5s. No dissolve, no fade, no whip pan, no transition effect — a straight in-camera cut, exactly like a creator cutting two takes together.

REAL TIME — NORMAL 1x SPEED. THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT RULE ABOUT THE PERSON IN THIS CLIP. Everything the woman does happens at the pace a real person actually moves. ABSOLUTELY NO fast motion, NO sped-up playback, NO double speed, NO time-lapse, NO hyperlapse, NO sudden jump in tempo, NO frantic twitchy limbs, NO snapping instantly from one pose to the next. There is DELIBERATELY very little for her to do. If the action does not fit comfortably, SLOW THE ACTION DOWN; never speed the footage up. It is far better that she is still mid-reach as the clip ends than that any part of it looks fast-forwarded.

REFERENCE IMAGES. Reference image 1 is the first frame of scene 1 — start there, unchanged, and it owns the room, the woman, the camera angle and the geometry. Reference image 2 is the CLOSED PRODUCT BOX: the artwork on the box in this video must match it exactly. Reference image 3 is the OPEN BOX INTERIOR: what she reveals in scene 2 must match it exactly.

THE WOMAN — she is exactly the woman in reference image 1 and never changes: an early-thirties East Asian woman with straight black hair worn in a high ponytail, in a cream cable-knit cardigan over a white tee and light blue jeans. Same hair, same skin, same clothes, first frame to last.

BOX SPEC — TRUE IN EVERY FRAME. A matte black rigid retail box, 55 cm wide x 42 cm tall x 10 cm deep. It is BIG — much wider than the span of two outstretched hands; each of her hands covers less than a quarter of the box width, and laid on the sofa seat it spans most of one cushion. Front panel top to bottom: a large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; a warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; a red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag in the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border showing a night house facade with a glowing pixel LED Christmas tree on the garage door; a bottom row of five small gold line icons with white captions. NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO die-cut cutout, NO product visible through the front, NO glowing lights showing through the cardboard. The box is closed and sealed until the moment she opens it in scene 2.

SCENE 1 — 0.0 to 2.5s. CAMERA: HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, portrait, a friend filming from just behind her left shoulder at HER SHOULDER HEIGHT, tilted down only very slightly — exactly where reference image 1 was taken. NOT above her head, NOT a top-down shot. NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO DOLLY. Handheld micro-shake only. She is at the LEFT EDGE of frame, partly cut off by it; the deep teal velvet sofa is directly IN FRONT of her, running diagonally away into depth and filling the right and bottom-right of frame, its empty seat cushions facing up. The room behind her — the dark walnut plank floor, the warm white plaster wall with the black-framed arched mirror, the brass arc floor lamp, the cream and rust cushions, the ivory chunky knit throw and the real green fir tree with warm-white lights and gold baubles at the right — stays exactly as it is and never changes.

THE WHOLE POINT OF SCENE 1: she carries the box round in front of the sofa, sets it flat on the seat cushion AND drops to her knees IN THE SAME CONTINUOUS MOTION. These are not two separate steps — the box going down and her body going down are one move.
0.0-0.9s — one unhurried step forward and to her right, her shoulders turning, the heavy box swinging slowly out in front of her; her black ponytail swings across and settles, her cream sleeve creases with the weight.
0.9-1.8s — she steps in front of the sofa and leans forward from the hips, lowering the box steadily toward the empty teal velvet seat cushion, arms extending, knees already bending underneath her. This lowering is SLOW and heavy — the box is big and awkward and she is being careful with it.
1.8-2.5s — the box settles flat and printed-side-up on the cushion with a soft compression of the velvet, her hands still on it, and in the same continuous motion her knees come down onto the dark walnut floor one after the other, her weight settling and her back rounding forward; she tucks a loose strand behind one ear and plants both palms flat on the box lid, chest rising with one excited breath.

HARD CUT AT 2.5s.

SCENE 2 — 2.5 to 5.0s. Direct continuation, same room, same second, same woman, same cream cardigan, same box in exactly the same place on the same teal velvet cushion. The camera has moved about 30 degrees round to her side — a slightly more side-on three-quarter angle, a little lower, closer to the level of the sofa seat, so we now see the lid edge in profile and read the pivot clearly. Still HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, handheld micro-shake only, NO ZOOM.

LID MECHANISM, GEOMETRICALLY. The box is a magnetic-closure flip-top. The hinge runs along the TOP LONG EDGE of the box — the far edge away from her, at the back. The lid pivots UP and BACK on that hinge, rotating away from her and toward the back of the sofa, keeping its printed front face turned toward the camera as it rises. The inside of the lid is matte black. The lid is NEVER lifted off, NEVER removed, NEVER carried aside, NEVER set down, NEVER folded flat instantly, NEVER slid out like a drawer.
2.5-3.1s — she hooks her fingertips under the near front edge of the lid; a small snap as the magnets release and the front edge lifts a centimetre, a thin black shadow line opening slowly across the whole 55 cm width.
3.1-4.3s — she pivots the big printed lid UP and BACK on its hinge in ONE slow continuous arc with real cardboard weight, her elbows rising and her shoulders lifting with the effort, until it stands roughly vertical. This arc takes well over a full second of screen time — it travels heavily and never flips or snaps open.
4.3-5.0s — the interior is revealed exactly as reference image 3: the neatly folded BLACK LED mesh curtain lying in its tray, the coiled black lead and the small accessory items beside it, all against a matte black interior. She reaches in with both hands and touches the folded black mesh, fingers spreading it slightly, then lifts her chin, looks off toward the fir tree and breaks into a big open-mouthed grin, eyebrows up.

MOVEMENT RULE FOR BOTH SCENES: she is never frozen and never hurried. Her body is in continuous unhurried motion the whole clip — weight shifting, chest breathing, ponytail and sleeves swinging and settling, the cushion compressing under the box — and her face keeps changing, eyes, eyebrows and mouth reading differently moment to moment. But every change FLOWS smoothly into the next at natural human speed. She never snaps, never jitters, never stutters between poses.

HARD RULES: exactly ONE cut, at 2.5s. The box never changes size, never changes artwork, never gains a window or a cutout. The curtain inside the box is BLACK cord with small dark bulbs — never white wire, never clear wire, never silver wire. Nobody else enters frame. ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Quiet warm living-room ambience, the soft thump of a heavy box on a cushion, knees on the floor, the click of magnets releasing, stiff cardboard flexing, plastic mesh rustling, one small delighted exhale.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC indoors at night. Warm mixed lamp and fairy light, slight sensor noise in the shadows, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp, no fast motion. The whole clip plays at normal 1x speed from first frame to last.
```

Down in the lower left of the lit area the GREEN GRINCH tiptoes on the spot on exaggerated pointed toes with SANTA'S BULGING RED SACK slung over his shoulder, grinning back over his shoulder with a wide toothy smirk, one wrapped present already tumbling out of the sack behind him. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, spinning, skidding, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. All their movement is in their bodies, not in their position.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. 0.0-0.5s — a child near the front is already bouncing on the spot and swings an arm up toward the garage mid-bounce; two adults beside them are mid-conversation, heads turned to each other, one shrugging.
0.5-1.0s — a woman in the centre laughs and rocks back on her heels, shoulders shaking; a man at the right is already filming on his phone and shifts his weight to his other foot; someone lifts a takeaway cup and drinks.
1.0-1.5s — the kids in the front row lean into each other and one points lazily at the lights without turning round; a teenager at the left shoves both hands deeper into their pockets, hunches, and blows out a long fog of breath.
1.5-2.0s — a woman at the left leans sideways into the person beside her and says something; they both laugh; another person scratches under their bobble hat and looks away from the garage entirely.
2.0-2.5s — two people knock shoulders and one claps once, casually; a child in a bobble hat twists round to tug a sleeve, then swings back; a couple sway slightly, arms linked.
2.5-3.0s — scattered easy laughter ripples through the group, a few loose claps at different moments, not together; one adult crouches down to say something to a kid; another points out at the lights with a cup still in hand.
3.0-3.5s — the man at the right lowers his phone, wipes his eyes laughing and says something sideways; a kid scrambles up onto their knees; two heads tip together.
3.5-4.0s — a few people applaud loosely, hats bobbing, breath fogging, someone waves an arm overhead at a neighbour, someone else is already turning to talk to the person behind them.
Coats crease, hats bob, breath fogs, snow compresses under boots, shoulders shake with laughter. NOBODY IS EVER STILL and nobody is waiting.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit garage door above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa, the Grinch, the sack and the star-burst is the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark garage door between the highest lit point of the artwork — Santa's flying red hat — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No fist, no boot, no green foot, no beard, no hat, no present and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the sage-green stucco, the gables, the roof, the sky, the spruce trees, the lamp post or the driveway. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, spinning, skidding, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. All their movement is in their bodies, not in their position.

THE LIGHT PICTURE IS A LITTLE CARTOON THAT PLAYS OUT, NOT A PICTURE THAT SITS THERE FLASHING. This is a WIDE shot seen from the bottom of a driveway, so small pose flicker inside the artwork will not read at all. What reads at this distance is movement that TRAVELS across the picture. FOUR layers run at the same time for the whole four seconds, and all four are big, obvious movements:
LAYER ONE — THE FIGHT ITSELF, in BIG whole-body redraws. Santa and the Grinch trade slaps, shoves, headlocks and tugs on the sack, both leaning right over, both fully changing shape between beats: arms swung across, bodies bent double, one dropped into a crouch while the other rears back. Between one beat and the next the silhouette of each figure is unmistakably a different shape — never a small wobble.
LAYER TWO — SANTA'S RED HAT FLIES BACK AND FORTH ACROSS THE TOP BAND. Knocked loose, it arcs from one side of the lit area to the other and back again across the four seconds, tumbling end over end as it goes, drawn by whole bulbs switching so it visibly somersaults rather than glides. It stays well below the top edge of the curtain at every moment and never leaves the rectangle. One of them swipes at it and misses each time it passes.
LAYER THREE — PRESENTS BOUNCE OUT ALONG THE BOTTOM BAND. Chunky wrapped presents in bright red, green, gold and blue burst out of the sack one after another and BOUNCE sideways along the very bottom of the picture, hopping in visible arcs, a new one every half second, so at any instant two or three presents are in the air at different heights. They bounce only inside the rectangle and never fall out of the bottom of the curtain; they gather into a pile at the far corners that grows visibly wider across the four seconds, spreading SIDEWAYS only, never upward.
LAYER FOUR — THE STAR-BURST PUMPS AND THE GOLD STARS STREAM. The white star-burst puff between them flashes and changes shape on every impact, and the scattered gold stars continuously chase around the pair, one lighting as the one before it goes out, an endless streaming ripple that never stops.

THE ANIMATION IS RELENTLESS FROM FRAME ONE. THE BIGGEST MISTAKE WOULD BE A QUIET START. At 0.0s the artwork is already mid-cycle, already loud, already changing, the hat is already mid-flight and two presents are already mid-bounce — the picture does NOT switch on, does NOT hold still, does NOT ease in. SOMETHING CHANGES EVERY THREE TENTHS OF A SECOND — at least THIRTEEN separate changes across the four seconds — and at every single instant AT LEAST THREE things are changing at once: a whole-body pose, the hat travelling, a present travelling, and a lighting effect somewhere else. There is no held frame, no pause, no settle:

0.0s — the clip opens mid-slap: Santa's arm already swung right across his body and the Grinch already bent backwards away from it, the star-burst firing bright and the hat tumbling left across the top band.
0.3s — the Grinch swings back with both long arms and Santa ducks into a low crouch, and a chase of colour runs across the whole picture from left to right.
0.6s — Santa lunges in and grabs the sack with both hands; the Grinch plants both feet and hauls the other way, the sack stretching long between them, and every gold star twinkles out and back.
0.9s — the Grinch lets go suddenly and Santa flies backwards onto his backside with both boots in the air, while the hat reverses direction and tumbles back the other way.
1.2s — Santa scrambles up on all fours and butts the Grinch in the stomach; the Grinch folds double with his eyes crossed, and the star-burst bursts to double size.
1.5s — they lock into a spinning tussle, arms tangled and both heads down, and the outline of both figures flashes brighter one bulb row at a time from the bottom upward.
1.8s — the Grinch gets Santa in a headlock and rubs his knuckles on Santa's head; Santa's beard flies out sideways and his legs kick, and two more presents burst out and bounce along the bottom.
2.1s — Santa stamps on the Grinch's bare green foot; the Grinch shoots straight up rigid with his mouth wide open and both arms pinned to his sides, flashing green.
2.4s — the Grinch hops on one foot clutching the other, and Santa snatches the sack back and hugs it to his chest, while a chase of colour runs UP the whole picture.
2.7s — the Grinch dives at the sack and they both tip over sideways in a heap, boots and long green legs sticking out, and the gold stars strobe.
3.0s — they pop back up nose to nose, both leaning in hard with their fists up, and the star-burst flashes between them in alternating colours.
3.3s — Santa swings a huge roundhouse slap that spins the Grinch right round on the spot twice, and the bouncing presents all flash colour at once.
3.6s — the Grinch, still spinning, catches the flying hat on his own head and freezes mid-spin looking at it, while Santa points at him with both hands and the picture flashes in alternating vertical strands.
3.9s — Santa grabs his hat back and jams it on, both of them slumped and panting with the sack held between them, and the whole picture — Santa, the Grinch, the sack, the presents, the star-burst and every gold star — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.

Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade, never a realistic animation curve. Coloured light spills and shifts across the driveway and over the backs of the crowd with every change.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the surface behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, a loose crowd chatting and laughing at different moments, scattered uneven clapping, boots in snow, breath fogging. No commentary, no voice-over.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.

#### Why C4–C7 were blocked the first time — artwork, not prompts

All four clips that carry light artwork came back `ip_detected`. The two that went through — C2 (the
unboxing) and C3 (the unlit install) — are the only two shots in this ad with **no character artwork in
them at all**. The split is exact and it is not random.

The cause is that video 27's light artwork uses **the Grinch**, a Dr. Seuss character, across all four
shots: Santa head-first down the chimney with the Grinch sneaking off with the sack (C4 and C5), Santa
and the Grinch fighting over the sack (C6), and Santa twerking with the Grinch facepalming (C7). This is
a genuine protected-character block on the provider's side, not the transient `ip_detected` glitch that
v28 C4 hit.

**These four must not be retried as-is, and the prompts must not be reworded to describe the character
without naming him** — describing a known character by its features is the same request, and working
around the detection is not an option.

The fix was to replace the Grinch with an original character — done in the rebuild above. Note that only
**three** start frames contained him (`v27_c05_g1.png`, `v27_c06_g1.png`, `v27_c07_g1.png`); C4's frame is
the unlit garage and needed no regeneration.

---

#### Containment and motion

Daniel: *"the animations must not break the frame where they are placed and must contain a lot of
motion and be super engaging and interesting"*.

All four artwork prompts carry the size-lock block — the artwork is the same number of bulbs tall and
wide in the last frame as in the first, with at least six unlit bulb rows of margin on every side, and
*"if a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER"*, plus
*"BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS."* Motion is at least
thirteen beats at 0.3s with two layers changing at once. That work carries over unchanged to the rewrite —
only the character needs replacing.

---

#### Take 3 — C4 sync + "looks like an LED panel", and the dead opening second in C5 and C7

Daniel rejected three v27 clips: C4 for bad sync and artwork that "looks completely fake, like it's on
an LED panel, not coming from our lights", and C5 and C7 for having no motion in the animation at the
start of the video. Each was diagnosed off ffmpeg contact sheets of the actual footage rather than
patched by feel.

**C4, the sync.** On the rejected take the hand and the phone stayed in shot for the entire four
seconds and the wall lit at roughly frame 13 (~1.05s) with no visible tap anywhere — the press and the
light-up were completely decoupled, so there was nothing for a viewer to read as cause and effect. The
old prompt already said the hand exits; the model simply ignored a buried sentence. Three changes: the
press is now staged as a long, clearly visible thumb travel down the screen landing squarely on the
play button, which visibly depresses with its blue ring flashing white; the wall is explicitly dark in
the frame *before* the thumb touches and fully lit in the frame it touches; and the hand exit was
promoted out of the body text into its own top-level HARD RULE with a stated failure condition —
*"If the phone is still visible at 1.0s, THE CLIP HAS FAILED."*

**C4, the LED-panel look.** The artwork rendered as a solid saturated blue rectangle with a white snow
band across it — which is exactly the failure `content/curtain_realism/anti_panel_prompt_block.md`
names as the single biggest tell ("the saturated blue 'sky' behind the characters"). The root cause was
process, not wording: **that block existed in the project but had never been pasted into any video
prompt** — only a one-paragraph summary of it had. The full five-paragraph block is now pasted verbatim
into the C4 prompt, plus a dedicated paragraph stating that the sky behind the characters is not drawn
at all and stays unlit gable wall.

**C5 and C7, the dead first second.** Root cause was an instruction that is *impossible to satisfy*:
the prompts said the animation is "already ON, already mid-move in the VERY FIRST FRAME" — but frame
one **is** the start image, so the only way the model can comply is to hold it and then ease in, which
is precisely the failure. Replaced with something checkable: freeze at 0.2s, 0.4s and 0.6s and those
must be three completely different drawings; the opening pose is abandoned within two frames and never
returns; no ease-in, ramp-u

---

## Prompt-file versions not on the board

These are the prompts as they sit in `video_prompts/`. Where a shot above shows a different text, this is a different pass at the same shot - usually a later rewrite - so both are kept.

### `v27_c04.txt`

```text
Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same night, same DETACHED TWO-CAR GARAGE seen dead-on and flat, same wide CHARCOAL-GREY sectional double garage door with its panel seams and its row of small square windows across the top, same warm white board-and-batten siding, same black gutter and black downpipe on the right, same black carriage lamp glowing either side of the door, same white picket fence at the left, same snow-covered driveway in the foreground, same hand coming up from the bottom holding a phone up big in the lower part of frame, same app screen showing a dark navy grid of small colourful scene tiles with a BIG ROUND BLUE PLAY BUTTON near the bottom of the screen.

THE CURTAIN IS UNLIT IN FRAME ONE AND IT STAYS UNLIT UNTIL THE PRESS. In the reference image the LED curtain hanging across the garage door is completely switched off — it is almost invisible, and the door simply reads as a plain dark charcoal garage door exactly as in the reference. That is what frame one of this video looks like. There is NO picture in the lights at 0.0s. There is NO colour, NO glow, NO artwork, NO character, NO animation and NO light of any kind on that door until the button is pressed.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, handheld micro-shake only. The camera stays DEAD-ON SQUARE to the garage door the whole four seconds — it never swings round to an oblique angle, never turns to a corner view, never tilts up. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY. The garage and the phone stay exactly the same size in frame throughout.

THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP IS THE SYNC. The thumb presses the big round PLAY button, and ON THAT VERY SAME FRAME the garage door lights up. Not a frame early, not a frame late, no gap, no delay, no anticipation, no lag. The press and the light are ONE event. And the instant the button is pressed the hand immediately drops away out of the bottom of frame — it does not linger, does not hold the phone up to admire the door, does not stay in shot for even half a second after the press.

THE PHONE PART IS SHORT. THE LIT PART IS MOST OF THE CLIP. The phone is finished and gone by 1.3s. From 1.3s to the very last frame — nearly THREE FULL SECONDS — there is nothing in the foreground and the door is lit and animating hard. The animation must NOT be squeezed into the last second and must NOT be a slow, sparse, gentle thing.

ACTION TIMELINE (4 seconds, one continuous take, no cuts):
0.0-0.8s — THE DOOR IS DARK. A SHORT SCROLL, NOT A LONG ONE. The thumb comes in from the bottom and gives the grid of scene tiles ONE small flick: the tiles slide up by roughly ONE ROW only, a single fresh row of new scene thumbnails comes into view from the bottom, the list overshoots slightly and settles back with a small bounce. That is the entire scroll. NO long scroll, NO repeated flicks, NO second flick, NO rows racing past, NO scrolling through the whole list. One tile in the grid keeps its bright highlight ring the whole time — the scene is already chosen.
0.8-1.0s — THE DOOR IS STILL DARK. The thumb slides straight down to the BIG ROUND BLUE PLAY BUTTON at the bottom of the app screen and comes to rest on it.
1.0s — THE THUMB PRESSES PLAY. On that exact frame the button visibly depresses, its blue ring flashes white, AND the entire garage door SNAPS ON at full brightness in the very same single frame. NO fade, NO dissolve, NO ramp, NO brightness curve, NO bulbs lighting one by one, NO sweep across the door, NO pause between the press and the light. One frame dark, the next frame fully lit at maximum brightness.
THE ARTWORK THAT APPEARS: a big jolly white-bearded SANTA in his red fur-trimmed suit and red hat, seen from the front, wearing dark sunglasses, lounging back in a steaming round wooden HOT TUB that fills the lower half of the lit area. One arm is raised, the other hand holds a tall cocktail with a tiny umbrella in it. Fat round bubbles rise and pop around him, curls of white steam lift off the water, and a few small gold stars and white snowflakes are scattered in the dark around the tub. Colours are bright red, white, warm gold, pale blue water and a little green on the umbrella, against the dark unlit door. There is no chimney, no goblin, no sack and no presents in this clip.
1.0-1.3s — the hand drops straight down and out of the bottom of frame fast and NEVER returns. The phone, the hand, the fingers and the sleeve are completely gone from frame by 1.3s and no part of them reappears at any point.
1.3-4.0s — THE REST OF THE CLIP IS THE LIT DOOR ALONE, and it is relentless. See the movement block below.

THE SKY BEHIND HIM IS NOT DRAWN. There is NO blue background, NO coloured backdrop, NO filled sky, NO solid panel of colour behind the figure. Behind and around Santa and the tub the curtain is simply UNLIT — dark charcoal garage door showing through, with the unlit bulbs reading as tiny dull grey dots and the black cords as faint vertical lines. Only Santa, the tub, the water, the bubbles, the steam, the stars and the snowflakes are lit. A saturated rectangle of background colour is the single clearest sign this is a video screen rather than a light curtain, and it must not appear anywhere.

MOVEMENT — RELENTLESS, AND IT NEVER STOPS. From the moment the door snaps on, SOMETHING CHANGES EVERY THREE TENTHS OF A SECOND — that is at least NINE separate changes between 1.3s and the last frame — and at every single instant AT LEAST TWO things are changing at once: Santa's whole-body pose AND a lighting effect somewhere else in the picture. There is no held pose, no still frame, no pause, no settle, no gentle drifting. Every pose is a clearly different WHOLE-BODY pose, the figure fully redrawing between beats like an animated light-bulb sign.
1.3s — he lands out of the snap-on already mid-move, the cocktail swung up in front of his chest and tipped toward the viewer, cheeks ballooned out fat mid-slurp, and the round bubbles fire on one after another around the rim of the tub.
1.6s — he throws both arms out level to the sides with his elbows bent, head tipped back, mouth a big open O, while the water in the tub chases colour left to right.
1.9s — he twists hard to one side, one shoulder driven forward, the cocktail held out level, and his sunglasses flash bright white twice.
2.2s — he twists hard the other way, the other shoulder forward, his beard swinging across, and a bright chase of colour runs down his whole body from hat to hands.
2.5s — he drops down low into the water until only his eyes, his hat and the raised cocktail are left showing, and every bubble twinkles out and back in a rolling wave from left to right.
2.8s — he pops straight back up with both arms braced on the rim of the tub and shimmies his shoulders left-right-left, the curls of steam flashing alternately.
3.1s — he kicks one boot up out of the water low and short to the side, arms bent and swinging opposite, his eyes wide open circles, and the wooden staves of the tub swap colour warm brown to red.
3.4s — he claps both hands together in front of his chest twice fast, his head bobbing with each clap, and the white fur trim on his suit and hat flashes brighter.
3.7s — he bounces in the tub twice, both arms bent up beside his head, the whole figure squashing and stretching with each bounce, and the stars and snowflakes flash on and off in alternating pairs.
3.9s — he lands square, cocktail raised, and the whole picture — figure, tub, water, bubbles, steam, stars and snowflakes — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.
Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow below with every change.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE. When the door snaps on, the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit door above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from 1.0s to the last frame. Santa and the tub are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the frame they first appear. He NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps upward, never fills more of the curtain than he does at the start. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark door between the highest lit point of the artwork and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No hand, no hat, no cocktail, no bubble, no droplet, no steam wisp and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain, and nothing lit ever appears on the white siding, the black gutter, the roof, the sky, the picket fence or the driveway. If a pose would raise him or widen him past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE HE CANNOT GROW, ALL HIS ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, sinking, leaning, bouncing, kicking low, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. He also stays ON THE SPOT and centred; he never walks or slides sideways across the curtain.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT. The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights: many thin vertical BLACK wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.
The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the garage door behind it: its panel seams, its trim, the row of small square windows, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin BLACK wires as faint vertical lines.
Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.
The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.
ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN: FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, a big silly cartoon face. It is NOT a photograph, NOT a realistic person, NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the door. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

HARD RULES: NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork, and no captions, subtitles or watermark anywhere in the video. Nobody walks into frame.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Quiet cold suburban night ambience, a faint distant car, one soft UI tap on the press, a faint electrical buzz as the door comes on. No voice.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC at night. Noisy shadows, slight motion blur in the highlights, natural phone screen glow on the fingers, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.
```

He is NOT tall, NOT lanky, NOT thin, NOT furry and NOT a shaggy green creature — he is a squat pot-bellied little goblin in a maroon hat and a striped scarf.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. If any figure could be mistaken for a photo of something real, it is wrong. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CAMERA — FIRST-PERSON POV. The person filming is WALKING FORWARD up the shovelled path toward the house, one continuous four-second take. THE CAMERA ITSELF IS THE PHONE, so we see ONLY what the walker sees. The house grows steadily and continuously larger and NEVER recedes. Natural bodyweight rise-and-fall with each stride, a slight left-right sway, strong parallax — the low stone border, the footprints and the snow banks at the edges of frame sweep past and out of frame much faster than the house moves. THIS IS WALKING, NOT ZOOMING: no focal length change, no digital zoom, no push-in, no dolly rig smoothness, no gimbal glide, no drone move. Handheld, breathing, slightly imperfect.

HARD NEGATIVE — NO PHONE IN SHOT. There is no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no glove, no device and no object of any kind in the foreground or along the bottom edge at any moment.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit brick wall above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa's boots, the chimney and the goblin is the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark brick wall between the highest lit point of the artwork — the tip of Santa's kicking boots — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No boot, no brick, no sack, no present, no snowflake and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the white trim, the white window frames, the porch, the roof, the sky, the stone border or the neighbouring houses. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, spinning, skidding, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. All their movement is in their bodies, not in their position. As the camera walks closer the bulb grid simply becomes coarser and the individual bulbs become bigger and more countable — the artwork does not gain detail and does not gain size.

MOVEMENT — THE FIRST SECOND IS THE MOST IMPORTANT SECOND OF THIS CLIP. The one failure that would ruin this shot is an artwork that holds its opening pose through the first second and only then eases into motion. That is exactly what must not happen.

THE CHECKABLE RULE: freeze this clip at 0.2s, again at 0.4s and again at 0.6s. Those three frames must be THREE COMPLETELY DIFFERENT DRAWINGS — different limb positions, different silhouettes, different lit shapes in different places. If any two of them could be mistaken for the same picture, or if any one of them still looks like the opening frame, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.

BY 0.2 SECONDS the artwork is already a visibly different drawing from frame one. The opening pose is ABANDONED WITHIN TWO FRAMES and never returns. There is NO ease-in, NO ramp-up, NO slow start, NO settling, NO warm-up, NO micro-wobble while the animation gets going, NO gentle drifting at the top of the clip.

THE FIRST SECOND RUNS AT DOUBLE SPEED: a brand new whole-body pose at 0.1s, 0.25s, 0.4s, 0.55s, 0.7s, 0.85s and 1.0s — SEVEN complete pose changes inside the first second alone, so the first second is the BUSIEST second of the clip, not the quietest. From 1.0s onward a new whole-body pose every three tenths of a second to the very last frame, and it is STILL running hard in the last frame. At every single instant AT LEAST TWO things are changing at once: a whole-body pose AND a lighting effect somewhere else in the picture. There is no held pose, no still frame, no pause, no settle. Every beat is a clearly DIFFERENT WHOLE-BODY pose — arms, legs, head and body all in a new position — the figures fully redrawing between beats like an animated light-bulb sign. If two consecutive beats look similar, it is wrong.

0.1s — it is already mid-move: Santa's boots scissoring wide apart above the chimney, knees bent, while the soot puff flashes bright and the goblin's toothy grin flips wider.
0.25s — both boots kick down and together hard, the whole chimney rocking sideways with the impact, and a bright chase of colour runs across the brickwork from left to right.
0.4s — one leg bends right over sideways and the other shoots out straight, a black boot flying off and spinning in the air beside it, while every snowflake twinkles out and back in a rolling wave.
0.55s — the legs pedal like a bicycle, the chimney squashing down short and wide underneath them, and the goblin lifts one knobbly green knee high in an exaggerated tiptoe step, his whole body flashing bright green.
0.7s — the goblin drops the sack and it thumps down on the bottom band, bursting a present out sideways, while Santa's boots knock together heel to heel twice fast.
0.85s — the legs cross over each other and lock, boots pointing in opposite directions, and the whole picture flashes brighter one bulb row at a time from the bottom upward.
1.0s — the goblin swings the red sack across his body and two more wrapped presents tumble out of it, somersaulting down and landing on the bottom band, while Santa's legs go rigid and shudder.
1.3s — the legs bend deep and heave, the chimney tilting hard the other way and chunky bricks popping loose around the rim, and a chase of colour runs UP from the bottom of the picture to the boots.
1.6s — one boot kicks straight out sideways and connects with the goblin's backside; the goblin shoots forward bent double with both arms flung out, and every gold star strobes with the kick.
1.9s — the goblin spins right round on the spot clutching the sack, hat whipping across, while Santa's legs windmill in a full circle and the soot puff bursts to double size.
2.2s — Santa's legs splay out flat to the sides like a starfish and slap back in, and the snowflakes flash on and off in alternating pairs.
2.5s — the goblin hauls the sack back up onto his shoulder with both hands and staggers backwards under the weight, while one of Santa's legs shoots straight up rigid and the chimney rim flashes white.
2.8s — the goblin drops into a crouch and peers up at the boots with one hand shading his eyes, the other still gripping the sack, while the brickwork strobes red and white.
3.1s — both boots stamp the air twice fast, the chimney bouncing under them, and the fallen presents on the bottom band flash colour one after another.
3.4s — Santa's legs knot themselves around each other and snap untangled, the soot puff blowing out sideways, while the goblin hops on one foot clutching a stubbed green toe.
3.7s — the goblin throws the sack over his other shoulder and skips on the spot with both knees up, while Santa's legs kick in opposite directions at once and the gold stars swap colour gold to white.
3.9s — Santa's boots snap wide apart in a big V, the goblin claps both hands over his mouth mid-laugh, and the whole picture — chimney, boots, sack, goblin, snowflakes and every gold star — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.

Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade, never a realistic animation curve. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow and the shovelled path with every change.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the surface behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, boots compressing snow at a walking rhythm, the faint electrical hum and tick of a big light display, one quiet breath. No voice.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.

He is SHORT AND SQUAT, only about half Santa's height, with a big round pot belly, spindly little arms and legs and bare knobbly bright LIME-GREEN feet. His skin is FLAT SMOOTH LIME GREEN — not furry, not fuzzy, not hairy, not shaggy, no fur texture of any kind. He has a long pointed chin, a wide lopsided grin with one crooked tooth, two small round YELLOW eyes and HUGE POINTED EARS sticking straight out sideways like leaves. He wears a floppy MAROON bobble hat pulled down over his ears and a long MUSTARD-AND-MAROON STRIPED SCARF trailing out behind him. He is NOT tall, NOT lanky, NOT thin, NOT furry and NOT a shaggy green creature — he is a squat pot-bellied little goblin in a maroon hat and a striped scarf.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. If any figure could be mistaken for a photo of something real, it is wrong. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, filmed from behind the group at the bottom of the driveway. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE. The house and the garage door stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

THE CROWD IS CASUAL, RELAXED AND ALREADY HAPPY — AND ALREADY MOVING IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME. This is not an audience waiting for something to happen. Nobody is standing frozen staring at the garage in anticipation, nobody holds still and then reacts, there is no build-up and no big synchronised cheer. They are neighbours hanging about on a driveway on a cold night, chatting to each other, laughing at each other, sipping drinks, glancing over at the lights and then back at their friends. Their attention drifts on and off the garage the whole time. They are ALL seen FROM BEHIND — no face is ever turned toward camera.
AT 0.0s, IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME, EVERY PERSON IS ALREADY MID-MOVEMENT. There is NO still opening frame, NO frozen first beat, NO pause before the motion starts. Someone is already mid-laugh, someone is already turning, someone is already raising a cup, a child is already bouncing. The clip starts in the middle of the action and never settles.
CONTINUOUS OVERLAPPING MOTION, EVERY HALF SECOND, NEVER IN UNISON — at any instant several different people are doing several different things, and their little actions start and stop at different times so the group always looks alive:
0.0-0.5s — a child near the front is already bouncing on the spot and swings an arm up toward the garage mid-bounce; two adults beside them are mid-conversation, heads turned to each other, one shrugging.
0.5-1.0s — a woman in the centre laughs and rocks back on her heels, shoulders shaking; a man at the right is already filming on his phone and shifts his weight to his other foot; someone lifts a takeaway cup and drinks.
1.0-1.5s — the kids in the front row lean into each other and one points lazily at the lights without turning round; a teenager at the left shoves both hands deeper into their pockets, hunches, and blows out a long fog of breath.
1.5-2.0s — a woman at the left leans sideways into the person beside her and says something; they both laugh; another person scratches under their bobble hat and looks away from the garage entirely.
2.0-2.5s — two people knock shoulders and one claps once, casually; a child in a bobble hat twists round to tug a sleeve, then swings back; a couple sway slightly, arms linked.
2.5-3.0s — scattered easy laughter ripples through the group, a few loose claps at different moments, not together; one adult crouches down to say something to a kid; another points out at the lights with a cup still in hand.
3.0-3.5s — the man at the right lowers his phone, wipes his eyes laughing and says something sideways; a kid scrambles up onto their knees; two heads tip together.
3.5-4.0s — a few people applaud loosely, hats bobbing, breath fogging, someone waves an arm overhead at a neighbour, someone else is already turning to talk to the person behind them.
Coats crease, hats bob, breath fogs, snow compresses under boots, shoulders shake with laughter. NOBODY IS EVER STILL and nobody is waiting.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit garage door above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa, the goblin, the sack and the star-burst is the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark garage door between the highest lit point of the artwork — Santa's flying red hat — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No fist, no boot, no green foot, no beard, no hat, no present and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the sage-green stucco, the gables, the roof, the sky, the spruce trees, the lamp post or the driveway. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, spinning, skidding, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. All their movement is in their bodies, not in their position.

THE LIGHT PICTURE IS A LITTLE CARTOON THAT PLAYS OUT, NOT A PICTURE THAT SITS THERE FLASHING. This is a WIDE shot seen from the bottom of a driveway, so small pose flicker inside the artwork will not read at all. What reads at this distance is movement that TRAVELS across the picture. FOUR layers run at the same time for the whole four seconds, and all four are big, obvious movements:
LAYER ONE — THE FIGHT ITSELF, in BIG whole-body redraws. Santa and the goblin trade slaps, shoves, headlocks and tugs on the sack, both leaning right over, both fully changing shape between beats: arms swung across, bodies bent double, one dropped into a crouch while the other rears back. Between one beat and the next the silhouette of each figure is unmistakably a different shape — never a small wobble.
LAYER TWO — SANTA'S RED HAT FLIES BACK AND FORTH ACROSS THE TOP BAND. Knocked loose, it arcs from one side of the lit area to the other and back again across the four seconds, tumbling end over end as it goes, drawn by whole bulbs switching so it visibly somersaults rather than glides. It stays well below the top edge of the curtain at every moment and never leaves the rectangle. One of them swipes at it and misses each time it passes.
LAYER THREE — PRESENTS BOUNCE OUT ALONG THE BOTTOM BAND. Chunky wrapped presents in bright red, green, gold and blue burst out of the sack one after another and BOUNCE sideways along the very bottom of the picture, hopping in visible arcs, a new one every half second, so at any instant two or three presents are in the air at different heights. They bounce only inside the rectangle and never fall out of the bottom of the curtain; they gather into a pile at the far corners that grows visibly wider across the four seconds, spreading SIDEWAYS only, never upward.
LAYER FOUR — THE STAR-BURST PUMPS AND THE GOLD STARS STREAM. The white star-burst puff between them flashes and changes shape on every impact, and the scattered gold stars continuously chase around the pair, one lighting as the one before it goes out, an endless streaming ripple that never stops.

THE ANIMATION IS RELENTLESS FROM FRAME ONE. THE BIGGEST MISTAKE WOULD BE A QUIET START. At 0.0s the artwork is already mid-cycle, already loud, already changing, the hat is already mid-flight and two presents are already mid-bounce — the picture does NOT switch on, does NOT hold still, does NOT ease in. SOMETHING CHANGES EVERY THREE TENTHS OF A SECOND — at least THIRTEEN separate changes across the four seconds — and at every single instant AT LEAST THREE things are changing at once: a whole-body pose, the hat travelling, a present travelling, and a lighting effect somewhere else. There is no held frame, no pause, no settle:

0.0s — the clip opens mid-slap: Santa's arm already swung right across his body and the goblin already bent backwards away from it, the star-burst firing bright and the hat tumbling left across the top band.
0.3s — the goblin swings back with both spindly arms and Santa ducks into a low crouch, and a chase of colour runs across the whole picture from left to right.
0.6s — Santa lunges in and grabs the sack with both hands; the goblin plants both feet and hauls the other way, the sack stretching long between them, and every gold star twinkles out and back.
0.9s — the goblin lets go suddenly and Santa flies backwards onto his backside with both boots in the air, while the hat reverses direction and tumbles back the other way.
1.2s — Santa scrambles up on all fours and butts the goblin in the stomach; the goblin folds double with his eyes crossed, and the star-burst bursts to double size.
1.5s — they lock into a spinning tussle, arms tangled and both heads down, and the outline of both figures flashes brighter one bulb row at a time from the bottom upward.
1.8s — the goblin gets Santa in a headlock and rubs his knuckles on Santa's head; Santa's beard flies out sideways and his legs kick, and two more presents burst out and bounce along the bottom.
2.1s — Santa stamps on the goblin's bare green foot; the goblin shoots straight up rigid with his mouth wide open and both arms pinned to his sides, flashing green.
2.4s — the goblin hops on one foot clutching the other, and Santa snatches the sack back and hugs it to his chest, while a chase of colour runs UP the whole picture.
2.7s — the goblin dives at the sack and they both tip over sideways in a heap, boots and stubby green legs sticking out, and the gold stars strobe.
3.0s — they pop back up nose to nose, both leaning in hard with their fists up, and the star-burst flashes between them in alternating colours.
3.3s — Santa swings a huge roundhouse slap that spins the goblin right round on the spot twice, and the bouncing presents all flash colour at once.
3.6s — the goblin, still spinning, catches the flying hat on his own head and freezes mid-spin looking at it, while Santa points at him with both hands and the picture flashes in alternating vertical strands.
3.9s — Santa grabs his hat back and jams it on, both of them slumped and panting with the sack held between them, and the whole picture — Santa, the goblin, the sack, the presents, the star-burst and every gold star — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.

Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade, never a realistic animation curve. Coloured light spills and shifts across the driveway and over the backs of the crowd with every change.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the surface behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, a loose crowd chatting and laughing at different moments, scattered uneven clapping, boots in snow, breath fogging. No commentary, no voice-over.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.
```

### `v27_c07.txt`

```text
THE FIRST FRAME OF THE VIDEO IS THE REFERENCE IMAGE, PIXEL FOR PIXEL, UNCHANGED. Do not redraw it, do not restage it, do not re-light it, do not change the lens or the angle. Start from that exact frame and animate forward from it: same night, same view shot from inside a moving car through the open passenger side window with the dark window frame at the edge of the frame, same wet reflective road with long smeared coloured reflections, same snowy verge, same street lamp, same row of neighbouring houses glowing down the street, same parked dark green estate car at the kerb, same row of snow-topped bins at the right edge, same lit LED curtain on the DARK CHOCOLATE-BROWN SHINGLED GABLE with cream trim showing SANTA TWERKING — a full-height chunky cartoon Santa seen FROM BEHIND and facing away from us, fully dressed in his red suit and black boots, knees bent deep in a wide squat, hands planted on his knees, back arched and his big round red backside popped out mid-bounce, his black belt swinging out to one side and the white bobble of his hat flicked forward over his shoulder, two chunky curved motion arcs either side of his hips, and beside him at the right, much smaller, the GREEN GOBLIN with one long green hand clapped over his eyes and the other flung up in despair, with small gold stars scattered around them.

CAMERA — THE CAR IS DRIVING FORWARDS. THIS IS THE THING THAT WENT WRONG LAST TIME: THE LAST VERSION LOOKED LIKE THE CAR WAS REVERSING. IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT INSTRUCTION IN THIS PROMPT.
The camera is looking out of the side window, across the road at the gable. The car is rolling FORWARDS down the street, parallel to the fronts of the houses — it drives PAST them, it does not drive AT them and it never backs up. Two physical facts define forwards, and both of them must be visible:

FACT ONE — EVERYTHING GETS BIGGER AS IT CROSSES THE FRAME, NOTHING EVER GETS SMALLER. This is the whole test. When a car drives forwards, objects come toward you: they appear small at the far end of the street, they GROW steadily as they approach, and they sweep out past the nearest edge of frame at their biggest. When a car reverses, objects SHRINK and retreat toward the distance. So: NOTHING in this clip ever shrinks. NOTHING ever retreats into the distance. NOTHING ever drifts back toward the far end of the street. NO PULL-BACK, NO ZOOM OUT, NO DOLLY BACK, NO RETREATING CAMERA. If any object in the frame gets smaller at any point, the car is reversing and THE SHOT HAS FAILED.

FACT TWO — THE FLOW IS ONE-WAY, LEFT TO RIGHT. Everything in the scene tracks steadily and continuously from the LEFT of frame toward the RIGHT of frame and out of the right edge. New scenery only ever ENTERS from the LEFT. NOTHING ever enters from the right edge. The direction never reverses, never stops, never stutters and is never played backwards.
- THE FOREGROUND SWEEPS PAST FAST AND GROWS AS IT GOES. The parked dark green estate car at the kerb, the snowy verge, the bins and the wet road in the bottom of frame all travel a LONG way to the right across the four seconds, getting visibly larger as they come nearer, and the parked green car swells and then sweeps out of the right edge and never comes back. That growing, sweeping foreground is the proof that the car is driving forwards.
- THE HOUSE IS FURTHER AWAY SO IT SLIDES MUCH MORE SLOWLY. The gable drifts to the right by roughly a quarter of the frame width over the whole clip and stays in frame the entire time. Strong parallax between the fast near ground and the slow far house is the entire point of the shot.
- The gable also comes very slightly NEARER as we approach it — its height on screen grows by a small amount across the clip. It grows a little; it must NEVER shrink. Because we are passing it, our angle onto the wall also opens out very slightly — by the end we are looking at it from a fraction further round than at the start.
- HARD NEGATIVE ON SCALE: the gable does not BALLOON either. It does not rush at the lens and it never grows big enough to fill most of the frame. There is NO zoom, NO focal length change, NO digital zoom, NO push-in at the wall, NO crabbing sideways toward the house, NO gimbal glide, NO drone move. The only reason anything changes size is that the car is rolling past it.
- Real vehicle motion the whole time: gentle suspension rock, slight rolling-shutter wobble on the lit bulbs, natural handheld drift, the operator making small corrections to keep the lit wall in frame. One single continuous unbroken take, no cuts, no jumps, no resets, and the footage is never reversed or played backwards.

HARD NEGATIVE — NO PHONE IN SHOT. There is no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no glove, no device and no object of any kind in the foreground or along the bottom edge at any moment. Only the car's own dark window frame at the edge, exactly as in the reference image.

THE GOBLIN — DRAW HIM EXACTLY AS DESCRIBED HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE. He is SHORT AND SQUAT, only about half Santa's height, with a big round pot belly, spindly little arms and legs and bare knobbly bright LIME-GREEN feet. His skin is FLAT SMOOTH LIME GREEN — not furry, not fuzzy, not hairy, not shaggy, no fur texture of any kind. He has a long pointed chin, a wide lopsided grin with one crooked tooth, two small round YELLOW eyes and HUGE POINTED EARS sticking straight out sideways like leaves. He wears a floppy MAROON bobble hat pulled down over his ears and a long MUSTARD-AND-MAROON STRIPED SCARF trailing out behind him. He is NOT tall, NOT lanky, NOT thin, NOT furry and NOT a shaggy green creature — he is a squat pot-bellied little goblin in a maroon hat and a striped scarf.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. Fully clothed cartoon figures throughout — no bare skin, no nudity; the joke is the pose and nothing more. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CONTINUOUS MOTION FROM THE VERY FIRST FRAME — NOT A SLIDESHOW OF POSES. THIS IS THE SECOND THING THAT WENT WRONG BEFORE.
The animation is ALREADY RUNNING AT FULL SPEED IN FRAME ONE. There is NO still opening frame, NO ease-in, NO ramp-up, NO slow start, NO settling, NO warm-up, NO micro-wobble while it gets going. Santa is mid-bounce at 0.0s and he is still bouncing in the last frame.
Santa moves the way a hand-drawn cartoon moves: every frame is an IN-BETWEEN of the frame before it. His hips, his knees, his shoulders and the bobble of his hat TRAVEL THROUGH each movement along a smooth arc — they are never teleported from one position to another, never cut, never snapped to a different unrelated drawing. There is no jump between unrelated pictures, no reset to the opening pose, no frozen hold, no pause, no settle and no loop restart at any point.
THE RHYTHM IS THE KEY: he is dancing to a beat, so the movement is a CONTINUOUS BOUNCE — down, up, down, up — running unbroken through the whole four seconds at roughly two full bounces per second, with the other business layered on top of that bounce without ever interrupting it. The bounce never stops, not for a single frame.
THE TEST: play any half second of this clip and the motion inside it must flow — his body carries through, his backside travels, the bobble swings on its arc. You must never see the picture blink from one drawing to a different unrelated drawing, and you must never see it hold still.
FLASHING IS NOT MOVEMENT. Colour chases and strobes are allowed but they are secondary — at every instant the thing a viewer sees is a BODY PHYSICALLY TRAVELLING, not lights blinking over a static shape.

THE DANCE, LAYERED ON TOP OF THE UNBROKEN BOUNCE:
0.0-0.5s — already mid-bounce and dropping lower with every beat: his backside pops right out and his knees drive wide, the two motion arcs pulsing in time, while the goblin's fingers spread slowly apart over his eyes.
0.5-1.0s — he starts swinging his hips side to side as he bounces, tracing a slow figure of eight, the black belt whipping across behind him and the white bobble swinging in a long arc over first one shoulder then the other.
1.0-1.5s — he takes one hand off his knee, sweeps it round in a wide slow arc and slaps his own backside on the beat, then brings it back — the arm travelling the whole way, never cutting.
1.5-2.0s — he walks it down: knees dipping alternately, hips sinking lower and lower on each bounce until he is squatting as low as he can go, the whole figure squashing short and wide at the bottom of the dip, while the goblin turns slowly away and hunches his shoulders up around his ears.
2.0-2.5s — he rises smoothly back up, still bouncing, and shimmies his shoulders left-right-left with both hands back on his knees, his beard swinging under his chin.
2.5-3.0s — he twists slowly a quarter turn so one shoulder comes round toward us, one boot lifting and pointing, then twists smoothly back square, the goblin peeking back through his fingers.
3.0-3.5s — he crosses one boot behind the other and grinds down low on the balls of his feet in one continuous sinking turn, the beard swinging out wide, the goblin's striped scarf whipping across him.
3.5-4.0s — he bounces backwards two steps with his backside leading and both hands paddling low at his sides, then drops into the deepest bounce of the whole clip, the goblin throwing both stubby arms out sideways in surrender — and the bounce is still going in the very last frame.

Every change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off across the grid — chunky and stepped, like an animated light-bulb sign — but the movement it describes is continuous and flowing, never a jump between unrelated drawings.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit gable wall above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa and the goblin are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark gable wall between the highest lit point of the artwork — the bobble of Santa's hat — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No boot, no belt, no bobble, no hand, no motion arc and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the roof, the cream trim, the window frames, the sky, the verge, the parked car or the neighbouring houses. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS AND DOWNWARDS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, grinding and swinging — never stretching upward. THE ARMS ARE NEVER RAISED OVERHEAD — Santa's hands stay on or below his knees and the goblin's raised hand stays below the top of his own head. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. (The wall does shift slowly across the frame because the car is driving past — that is the camera, not the artwork.)

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the brown shingled gable behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly wider than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular, never a square pixel matrix. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords — blown-out white core, coloured halo, individually countable. The unlit area is NOT a solid coloured field: it is simply dark gable wall with dull grey unlit bulbs and faint dark cords. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: real sound from inside a slowly rolling car at night — low engine hum, tyres hissing on wet tarmac, faint cold wind through the open window, a distant electrical hum from the display. NO music, NO score, NO background song, NO narration, NO voice-over, nobody speaks in this shot.

LOOK: real phone-camera night footage shot from inside a moving car, natural night exposure, mild sensor noise and grain, faint window reflections, no cinematic bokeh, no colour grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlays, no captions, no watermark, no logos.
