#### Image prompt — exact service text

```text
@Image 1 is a real photograph shot on an iPhone from inside a crowd of neighbours on a driveway, looking up at a man on a house roof. Reproduce it EXACTLY — the same house, the same grey shingle roof, the same stone-and-siding facade with the house number plaque, the same dark garage door, the same aluminium extension ladder running steeply up through the middle of the frame, the same man in the dark jacket and orange beanie standing on the roof, the same crowd of neighbours seen from behind filling the bottom third and cropped by both side edges with nobody facing the camera, the same overcast winter daylight, the same colours.

CHANGE EXACTLY TWO THINGS:

1. MORE CLEAN SKY AT THE TOP. Tilt the camera up very slightly and pull back a touch so that the TOP FIFTH of the vertical frame is completely empty flat overcast white-grey sky — no roof, no ladder, no people, no wires, nothing in that band. The roofline, the man and the inflatable all sit BELOW that empty band. The rest of the composition stays the same.

2. THE THING ON THE ROOF MUST CLEARLY READ AS A SHOP-BOUGHT AIR-BLOWN INFLATABLE SANTA CLAUS, WITH DUCT TAPE ON IT. It is a large half-inflated nylon yard Santa, sagging and slumped over on the shingles beside the man: an unmistakable bright RED coat and red trousers with WHITE fluffy fur trim at the cuffs, hem and hood, a WIDE BLACK BELT with a gold buckle across the belly, a big round WHITE BEARD and a pale printed face, a red pointed hat with a white pom-pom flopping sideways, and a round pot belly that is partly deflated so the nylon wrinkles and folds. It is obviously a cheap printed nylon inflatable, not a person and not a red blanket. Wrapped across it are several WIDE STRIPS OF SILVER DUCT TAPE — one long strip taped across its chest and over the shingles, another around its arm, another across its leg, plainly holding it down to the roof. The tape is clearly visible silver-grey and slightly crumpled. The man is leaning over it with one hand on the Santa as if pressing another strip of tape down. It must be large and readable — roughly half the frame width, in the upper-middle of the frame.

Everything else is identical to @Image 1. Do NOT change the crowd, do NOT turn any faces towards the camera, do NOT move the camera further back from the house, do NOT shrink the man.

Hyperrealistic amateur iPhone photograph, extremely high quality, perfect natural overcast daylight, real nylon and shingle texture, natural contact shadows, slight handheld imperfection. Not a render, not CGI, no text, no watermark, no logo.
```

#### Video prompt — `clips/v27_c01_g2.mp4` — IN THE CUT

**Settings** - seedance_2_5 · 720p · 5 s · 9:16 · audio on
**Job** - `f5f818fa-229d-48c0-b59f-3ffa8c9eeabf`
**Result** - https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260914_182817_f5f818fa-229d-48c0-b59f-3ffa8c9eeabf.mp4 (byte-identical to the local file, md5 checked 2026-09-23)
**Upscaled derivative** - `clips/upscaled/v27_c01_g2.mp4` (1440×2560)
**Attached, in submission order**
- start frame: `content/video_27_assets/starting_frames/v27_c01_g4.png` · Higgsfield media `2a8a1781-8118-43f4-8ec2-1cbcaf1d0690`

````text
The attached start frame is frame one. Animate it exactly as it is. Real documentary footage shot on a phone by a bystander in the crowd.

CAMERA: locked handheld. No zoom in, no zoom out, no push, no pull, no dolly, no pan, no tilt, no orbit, no crane, no rack focus, no reframe. The framing at 5.0s is identical to the framing at 0.0s. Only tiny involuntary handheld micro-jitter of one or two pixels. NOTHING of the person filming is visible at any moment - no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no device, nothing along the bottom edge.

TIMELINE, real time, everything obeys real gravity and real weight:
0.0-1.0s  The man is fighting with a roll of packing tape, trying to strap the sagging inflatable Christmas decoration down. He rips a length off, it curls and sticks to itself, he slaps it on and it peels straight back off. His jaw is clenched, his face is red, he is furious.
1.0-1.8s  He yanks on it with both hands, leaning his whole bodyweight back.
1.8-2.8s  His foot slips out from under him. He goes down hard and fast - real bodyweight, no hang time, no float - and at the same instant the inflatable tears free and topples DOWN THE SAME SIDE HE IS ON, the near side facing the camera. It deflates as it goes, the nylon wrinkling and sagging, and it slides down and lands on the ground right next to him, in the lower half of the frame, within arm's reach of his body. CRITICAL: the inflatable NEVER rises, NEVER floats, NEVER lifts into the sky, NEVER goes over to the far side away from him, NEVER leaves ground level. It falls straight down on his side and stays on the ground.
2.8-3.6s  He lands heavily, hip and shoulder first, dust and dry grass puffing up. The inflatable settles into a crumpled flat heap beside him, still twitching as the last air escapes.
3.6-5.0s  The neighbours in the foreground freeze - shoulders jolt, heads snap toward him, two of them flinch backwards, one raises a hand to their head. They stay seen FROM BEHIND for the whole clip; no face turns toward the camera at any point.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only - wind, tape ripping, a heavy thud of a body on ground, nylon collapsing, a grunt, and shocked gasps from the crowd: "Oh my God!" "Whoa!" No music, no score, no background song, no sound design.

LOOK - hyper realistic, photographic, indistinguishable from a real phone clip, bright midday sun: harsh real daylight with a slightly blown-out sky and crushed shadows, natural skin texture with pores and shine, hair moving in the wind, real cloth wrinkles, light motion blur on fast-moving limbs and fabric, faint rolling-shutter wobble, mild sensor noise in the shadows, one small autofocus hunt, slightly imperfect exposure. Comedic, nobody is hurt.
BANNED: animation, cartoon, illustration, stylised or synthetic imagery, plastic or waxy skin, weightless or floating motion, anything rising into the sky, rubbery limbs, objects sliding without friction, smooth interpolated motion, slow motion of any kind, glossy over-clean lighting, morphing faces or hands, extra fingers, warping straight lines, text, captions, watermark, logo.
````

````text
The attached reference photograph shows the SUBJECT AND THE ACTION only - a man up on a suburban garage roof wrestling an ugly half-deflated inflatable Christmas decoration, with an aluminium extension ladder leaning against the eave. Keep that subject and that action. DO NOT copy its camera: use a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT, MUCH WIDER ANGLE described below.

A real candid photograph taken on a recent iPhone, vertical 9:16, flat white overcast winter daylight.

THE NEW CAMERA: the photographer is standing far back across the street on the opposite kerb, holding the phone at chest height, roughly level with a slight upward tilt, shooting the house side-on at about a forty-five degree angle so we see the front of the house and the driveway running away to the right. It is a WIDE establishing shot: the whole house, the full driveway and a big crowd of people all fit in the frame. The TOP FIFTH OF THE FRAME IS CLEAN EMPTY FLAT WHITE OVERCAST SKY with nothing in it. The house roof line sits across the upper third. The bottom of the frame is the snowy road and kerb in the near foreground, slightly out of the way at the very bottom edge.

THE HOUSE: a two-storey suburban home with light grey stone-and-siding cladding, white trim, dark grey asphalt shingles, an attached garage with a black panelled door, a black wall lantern by the front door, a low grey stone wall along the edge of the driveway, bare trees behind.

THE MAN ON THE ROOF: a man in a charcoal grey puffer jacket, jeans and a mustard yellow knit beanie is up on the sloped garage roof, small in the frame because the camera is far back, but clearly readable. He is NOT falling and NOT off balance - he stands SAFELY AND SOLIDLY with BOTH BOOTS PLANTED FLAT on the shingles, knees bent, both hands gripping the inflatable, straining to haul it upright. An aluminium extension ladder leans against the eave beside him, its feet in the snow of the driveway.

THE DECORATION: a hideous sun-faded giant inflatable SANTA slumped over the roof ridge, half deflated so the belly sags and folds in deep wrinkles, the face bleached almost white with a crooked smeared painted smile, two grey duct-tape patches on the suit, the hat drooping down over one eye. It is strapped down with orange ratchet straps and wound in a nest of tangled multicolour bulb strings and limp silver tinsel. Its black cable runs off the roof and down to an orange extension lead in the snow. Obviously old, cheap and ugly.

THE CROWD - THE POINT OF THE SHOT: about TWENTY NEIGHBOURS have gathered on the driveway, the front lawn and the road in front of the house, all of them standing still and looking UP at the man on the roof. They are ordinary suburban people of mixed ages in ordinary winter clothes - puffer jackets in navy, olive, burgundy, cream and black, wool coats, beanies, scarves, a couple of hoods up, one man in a high-vis jacket, a woman holding a toddler on her hip, a teenager filming on a phone held up, two people with dogs on leads, an older couple arm in arm, a few holding takeaway coffee cups. MOST OF THEM ARE SEEN FROM BEHIND OR IN THREE-QUARTER REAR VIEW, heads tilted back looking up at the roof, so we mostly see backs of coats, hats and shoulders; only two or three at the edges are half-turned so a bit of profile shows. They are spread naturally across the driveway and spilling onto the road in loose clusters of two and three, not in a neat row and not a uniform mob, with real gaps between the groups and real footprints trampled in the snow around them. Two parked cars at the kerb, a red hatchback and a dark SUV, with a couple of people leaning against them watching.

This is the grim, embarrassing BEFORE shot in a before-and-after gag - the whole street has come out to watch a man struggle with a horrible old inflatable. Shot completely straight like a real phone snapshot.

IMAGE QUALITY - CRITICAL: a real photo taken on a recent iPhone in good flat overcast light. Extremely high quality, hyper realistic, tack-sharp across the whole frame from the foreground crowd to the roof, crisp shingle texture, crisp wrinkled vinyl fabric on the inflatable, crisp coat fabric and snow texture, correct human anatomy and correct number of limbs on every person. Absolutely no blur, no softness, no smearing, no heavy noise, no shallow-depth-of-field bokeh, no cinematic colour grade, no vignette, no haze, no bloom, no motion blur, no CGI polish, no illustration. Real amateur phone photo, natural noise. No text overlay, no captions, no watermark, no logos.

0.0-2.0s — she lifts the gathered bundle in her right hand and shakes it out ONCE, slowly and heavily. The shake takes well over a full second of screen time: her arm swings out and down, the tangled curtain lets go in stages rather than all at once, and a long sheet of black strands falls open DOWN THE CHARCOAL GARAGE DOOR and swings for a moment before it settles. The loose coil on the snow drags and shifts with it.
2.0-4.0s — still up on the stool, she stretches both arms high and presses the header wire up under the trim board directly above the garage door, her thumbs working slowly along it, then walks her hands a short way sideways to her RIGHT along the header, onto the bare stretch of door beyond the strands she has already hung, pressing and tucking as she goes, her torso twisting and her heels lifting and dropping on the tread. She is STILL tucking when the clip ends. The curtain sways gently against the door and is still settling on the last frame.
THAT IS THE WHOLE CLIP. She does NOT step down off the stool, does NOT step back, does NOT plant her hands on her hips and does NOT tilt her head back to look at her work. She stays on the stool from the first frame to the last.

THE LED CURTAIN IS BLACK — CRITICAL. The wire is BLACK, not white and not clear. Every vertical strand hanging down the garage door is a thin BLACK cord and the unlit bulbs on it are small and DARK. Against the charcoal door the strands still read as distinct dark lines with the door plainly visible between them, clearly visible the whole four seconds. The heaped bundle on the snow is a tangle of BLACK cord, clearly dark against the white snow. No white wire, no clear wire, no silver wire appears at any moment.

HARD RULES: the curtain is COMPLETELY OFF the entire time — not one bulb lights, no glow, no colour, no animation on the door. The garage never gains windows, doors or new features; the row of small square windows across the top of the door, the black gutter, the black downpipe on the right and the two carriage lamps never move; the white picket fence never changes. The strands are separate vertical strands obeying gravity, sagging slightly, with an uneven wavy bottom hem — never a flat sheet, never a panel, never a screen. The loose coil rests in the snow with real contact shadows. Her face is never shown; she stays turned away from camera throughout. No other people enter frame. ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor winter ambience, thin plastic strands rattling and clicking against a metal garage door, a step-stool creak, boots on a metal tread, boots compressing snow, one quiet effortful breath. No voice.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC. Flat overcast winter daylight, slightly cool white balance, mild sensor noise, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp, no fast motion. The whole clip plays at normal 1x speed from first frame to last.

Every beat is a clearly DIFFERENT WHOLE-BODY pose — arms, legs, head and body all in a new position — the figures fully redrawing between beats like an animated light-bulb sign. If two consecutive beats look similar, it is wrong.

0.1s — it is already mid-move: Santa's boots scissoring wide apart above the chimney, knees bent, while the soot puff flashes bright and the goblin's toothy grin flips wider.
0.25s — both boots kick down and together hard, the whole chimney rocking sideways with the impact, and a bright chase of colour runs across the brickwork from left to right.
0.4s — one leg bends right over sideways and the other shoots out straight, a black boot flying off and spinning in the air beside it, while every snowflake twinkles out and back in a rolling wave.
0.55s — the legs pedal like a bicycle, the chimney squashing down short and wide underneath them, and the goblin lifts one knobbly green knee high in an exaggerated tiptoe step, his whole body flashing bright green.
0.7s — the goblin drops the sack and it thumps down on the bottom band, bursting a present out sideways, while Santa's boots knock together heel to heel twice fast.
0.85s — the legs cross over each other and lock, boots pointing in opposite directions, and the whole picture flashes brighter one bulb row at a time from the bottom upward.
1.0s — the goblin swings the red sack across his body and two more wrapped presents tumble out of it, somersaulting down and landing on the bottom band, while Santa's legs go rigid and shudder.
1.3s — the legs bend deep and heave, the chimney tilting hard the other way and chunky bricks popping loose around the rim, and a chase of colour runs UP from the bottom of the picture to the boots.
1.6s — one boot kicks straight out sideways and connects with the goblin's backside; the goblin shoots forward bent double with both arms flung out, and every gold star strobes with the kick.
1.9s — the goblin spins right round on the spot clutching the sack, hat whipping across, while Santa's legs windmill in a full circle and the soot puff bursts to double size.
2.2s — Santa's legs splay out flat to the sides like a starfish and slap back in, and the snowflakes flash on and off in alternating pairs.
2.5s — the goblin hauls the sack back up onto his shoulder with both hands and staggers backwards under the weight, while one of Santa's legs shoots straight up rigid and the chimney rim flashes white.
2.8s — the goblin drops into a crouch and peers up at the boots with one hand shading his eyes, the other still gripping the sack, while the brickwork strobes red and white.
3.1s — both boots stamp the air twice fast, the chimney bouncing under them, and the fallen presents on the bottom band flash colour one after another.
3.4s — Santa's legs knot themselves around each other and snap untangled, the soot puff blowing out sideways, while the goblin hops on one foot clutching a stubbed green toe.
3.7s — the goblin throws the sack over his other shoulder and skips on the spot with both knees up, while Santa's legs kick in opposite directions at once and the gold stars swap colour gold to white.
3.9s — Santa's boots snap wide apart in a big V, the goblin claps both hands over his mouth mid-laugh, and the whole picture — chimney, boots, sack, goblin, snowflakes and every gold star — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.

Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade, never a realistic animation curve. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow and the shovelled path with every change.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. If any figure could be mistaken for a photo of something real, it is wrong. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, filmed from behind the group at the bottom of the driveway. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE. The house and the garage door stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

THE CROWD IS CASUAL, RELAXED AND ALREADY HAPPY — AND ALREADY MOVING IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME. This is not an audience waiting for something to happen. Nobody is standing frozen staring at the garage in anticipation, nobody holds still and then reacts, there is no build-up and no big synchronised cheer. They are neighbours hanging about on a driveway on a cold night, chatting to each other, laughing at each other, sipping drinks, glancing over at the lights and then back at their friends. Their attention drifts on and off the garage the whole time. They are ALL seen FROM BEHIND — no face is ever turned toward camera.
AT 0.0s, IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME, EVERY PERSON IS ALREADY MID-MOVEMENT. There is NO still opening frame, NO frozen first beat, NO pause before the motion starts. Someone is already mid-laugh, someone is already turning, someone is already raising a cup, a child is already bouncing. The clip starts in the middle of the action and never settles.
CONTINUOUS OVERLAPPING MOTION, EVERY HALF SECOND, NEVER IN UNISON — at any instant several different people are doing several different things, and their little actions start and stop at different times so the group always looks alive:
0.0-0.5s — a child near the front is already bouncing on the spot and swings an arm up toward the garage mid-bounce; two adults beside them are mid-conversation, heads turned to each other, one shrugging.
0.5-1.0s — a woman in the centre laughs and rocks back on her heels, shoulders shaking; a man at the right is already filming on his phone and shifts his weight to his other foot; someone lifts a takeaway cup and drinks.
1.0-1.5s — the kids in the front row lean into each other and one points lazily at the lights without turning round; a teenager at the left shoves both hands deeper into their pockets, hunches, and blows out a long fog of breath.
1.5-2.0s — a woman at the left leans sideways into the person beside her and says something; they both laugh; another person scratches under their bobble hat and looks away from the garage entirely.
2.0-2.5s — two people knock shoulders and one claps once, casually; a child in a bobble hat twists round to tug a sleeve, then swings back; a couple sway slightly, arms linked.
2.5-3.0s — scattered easy laughter ripples through the group, a few loose claps at different moments, not together; one adult crouches down to say something to a kid; another points out at the lights with a cup still in hand.
3.0-3.5s — the man at the right lowers his phone, wipes his eyes laughing and says something sideways; a kid scrambles up onto their knees; two heads tip together.
3.5-4.0s — a few people applaud loosely, hats bobbing, breath fogging, someone waves an arm overhead at a neighbour, someone else is already turning to talk to the person behind them.
Coats crease, hats bob, breath fogs, snow compresses under boots, shoulders shake with laughter. NOBODY IS EVER STILL and nobody is waiting.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, spinning, skidding, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. All their movement is in their bodies, not in their position.

THE LIGHT PICTURE IS A LITTLE CARTOON THAT PLAYS OUT, NOT A PICTURE THAT SITS THERE FLASHING. This is a WIDE shot seen from the bottom of a driveway, so small pose flicker inside the artwork will not read at all. What reads at this distance is movement that TRAVELS across the picture. FOUR layers run at the same time for the whole four seconds, and all four are big, obvious movements:
LAYER ONE — THE FIGHT ITSELF, in BIG whole-body redraws. Santa and the goblin trade slaps, shoves, headlocks and tugs on the sack, both leaning right over, both fully changing shape between beats: arms swung across, bodies bent double, one dropped into a crouch while the other rears back. Between one beat and the next the silhouette of each figure is unmistakably a different shape — never a small wobble.
LAYER TWO — SANTA'S RED HAT FLIES BACK AND FORTH ACROSS THE TOP BAND. Knocked loose, it arcs from one side of the lit area to the other and back again across the four seconds, tumbling end over end as it goes, drawn by whole bulbs switching so it visibly somersaults rather than glides. It stays well below the top edge of the curtain at every moment and never leaves the rectangle. One of them swipes at it and misses each time it passes.
LAYER THREE — PRESENTS BOUNCE OUT ALONG THE BOTTOM BAND. Chunky wrapped presents in bright red, green, gold and blue burst out of the sack one after another and BOUNCE sideways along the very bottom of the picture, hopping in visible arcs, a new one every half second, so at any instant two or three presents are in the air at different heights. They bounce only inside the rectangle and never fall out of the bottom of the curtain; they gather into a pile at the far corners that grows visibly wider across the four seconds, spreading SIDEWAYS only, never upward.
LAYER FOUR — THE STAR-BURST PUMPS AND THE GOLD STARS STREAM. The white star-burst puff between them flashes and changes shape on every impact, and the scattered gold stars continuously chase around the pair, one lighting as the one before it goes out, an endless streaming ripple that never stops.

THE ANIMATION IS RELENTLESS FROM FRAME ONE. THE BIGGEST MISTAKE WOULD BE A QUIET START. At 0.0s the artwork is already mid-cycle, already loud, already changing, the hat is already mid-flight and two presents are already mid-bounce — the picture does NOT switch on, does NOT hold still, does NOT ease in. SOMETHING CHANGES EVERY THREE TENTHS OF A SECOND — at least THIRTEEN separate changes across the four seconds — and at every single instant AT LEAST THREE things are changing at once: a whole-body pose, the hat travelling, a present travelling, and a lighting effect somewhere else. There is no held frame, no pause, no settle:

0.0s — the clip opens mid-slap: Santa's arm already swung right across his body and the goblin already bent backwards away from it, the star-burst firing bright and the hat tumbling left across the top band.
0.3s — the goblin swings back with both spindly arms and Santa ducks into a low crouch, and a chase of colour runs across the whole picture from left to right.
0.6s — Santa lunges in and grabs the sack with both hands; the goblin plants both feet and hauls the other way, the sack stretching long between them, and every gold star twinkles out and back.
0.9s — the goblin lets go suddenly and Santa flies backwards onto his backside with both boots in the air, while the hat reverses direction and tumbles back the other way.
1.2s — Santa scrambles up on all fours and butts the goblin in the stomach; the goblin folds double with his eyes crossed, and the star-burst bursts to double size.
1.5s — they lock into a spinning tussle, arms tangled and both heads down, and the outline of both figures flashes brighter one bulb row at a time from the bottom upward.
1.8s — the goblin gets Santa in a headlock and rubs his knuckles on Santa's head; Santa's beard flies out sideways and his legs kick, and two more presents burst out and bounce along the bottom.
2.1s — Santa stamps on the goblin's bare green foot; the goblin shoots straight up rigid with his mouth wide open and both arms pinned to his sides, flashing green.
2.4s — the goblin hops on one foot clutching the other, and Santa snatches the sack back and hugs it to his chest, while a chase of colour runs UP the whole picture.
2.7s — the goblin dives at the sack and they both tip over sideways in a heap, boots and stubby green legs sticking out, and the gold stars strobe.
3.0s — they pop back up nose to nose, both leaning in hard with their fists up, and the star-burst flashes between them in alternating colours.
3.3s — Santa swings a huge roundhouse slap that spins the goblin right round on the spot twice, and the bouncing presents all flash colour at once.
3.6s — the goblin, still spinning, catches the flying hat on his own head and freezes mid-spin looking at it, while Santa points at him with both hands and the picture flashes in alternating vertical strands.
3.9s — Santa grabs his hat back and jams it on, both of them slumped and panting with the sack held between them, and the whole picture — Santa, the goblin, the sack, the presents, the star-burst and every gold star — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.

Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade, never a realistic animation curve. Coloured light spills and shifts across the driveway and over the backs of the crowd with every change.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

CAMERA — THE CAR IS DRIVING FORWARDS. THIS IS THE THING THAT WENT WRONG LAST TIME: THE LAST VERSION LOOKED LIKE THE CAR WAS REVERSING. IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT INSTRUCTION IN THIS PROMPT.
The camera is looking out of the side window, across the road at the gable. The car is rolling FORWARDS down the street, parallel to the fronts of the houses — it drives PAST them, it does not drive AT them and it never backs up. Two physical facts define forwards, and both of them must be visible:

FACT ONE — EVERYTHING GETS BIGGER AS IT CROSSES THE FRAME, NOTHING EVER GETS SMALLER. This is the whole test. When a car drives forwards, objects come toward you: they appear small at the far end of the street, they GROW steadily as they approach, and they sweep out past the nearest edge of frame at their biggest. When a car reverses, objects SHRINK and retreat toward the distance. So: NOTHING in this clip ever shrinks. NOTHING ever retreats into the distance. NOTHING ever drifts back toward the far end of the street. NO PULL-BACK, NO ZOOM OUT, NO DOLLY BACK, NO RETREATING CAMERA. If any object in the frame gets smaller at any point, the car is reversing and THE SHOT HAS FAILED.

FACT TWO — THE FLOW IS ONE-WAY, LEFT TO RIGHT. Everything in the scene tracks steadily and continuously from the LEFT of frame toward the RIGHT of frame and out of the right edge. New scenery only ever ENTERS from the LEFT. NOTHING ever enters from the right edge. The direction never reverses, never stops, never stutters and is never played backwards.
- THE FOREGROUND SWEEPS PAST FAST AND GROWS AS IT GOES. The parked dark green estate car at the kerb, the snowy verge, the bins and the wet road in the bottom of frame all travel a LONG way to the right across the four seconds, getting visibly larger as they come nearer, and the parked green car swells and then sweeps out of the right edge and never comes back. That growing, sweeping foreground is the proof that the car is driving forwards.
- THE HOUSE IS FURTHER AWAY SO IT SLIDES MUCH MORE SLOWLY. The gable drifts to the right by roughly a quarter of the frame width over the whole clip and stays in frame the entire time. Strong parallax between the fast near ground and the slow far house is the entire point of the shot.
- The gable also comes very slightly NEARER as we approach it — its height on screen grows by a small amount across the clip. It grows a little; it must NEVER shrink. Because we are passing it, our angle onto the wall also opens out very slightly — by the end we are looking at it from a fraction further round than at the start.
- HARD NEGATIVE ON SCALE: the gable does not BALLOON either. It does not rush at the lens and it never grows big enough to fill most of the frame. There is NO zoom, NO focal length change, NO digital zoom, NO push-in at the wall, NO crabbing sideways toward the house, NO gimbal glide, NO drone move. The only reason anything changes size is that the car is rolling past it.
- Real vehicle motion the whole time: gentle suspension rock, slight rolling-shutter wobble on the lit bulbs, natural handheld drift, the operator making small corrections to keep the lit wall in frame. One single continuous unbroken take, no cuts, no jumps, no resets, and the footage is never reversed or played backwards.

HARD NEGATIVE — NO PHONE IN SHOT. There is no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no glove, no device and no object of any kind in the foreground or along the bottom edge at any moment. Only the car's own dark window frame at the edge, exactly as in the reference image.

THE GOBLIN — DRAW HIM EXACTLY AS DESCRIBED HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE. He is SHORT AND SQUAT, only about half Santa's height, with a big round pot belly, spindly little arms and legs and bare knobbly bright LIME-GREEN feet. His skin is FLAT SMOOTH LIME GREEN — not furry, not fuzzy, not hairy, not shaggy, no fur texture of any kind. He has a long pointed chin, a wide lopsided grin with one crooked tooth, two small round YELLOW eyes and HUGE POINTED EARS sticking straight out sideways like leaves. He wears a floppy MAROON bobble hat pulled down over his ears and a long MUSTARD-AND-MAROON STRIPED SCARF trailing out behind him. He is NOT tall, NOT lanky, NOT thin, NOT furry and NOT a shaggy green creature — he is a squat pot-bellied little goblin in a maroon hat and a striped scarf.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. Fully clothed cartoon figures throughout — no bare skin, no nudity; the joke is the pose and nothing more. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CONTINUOUS MOTION FROM THE VERY FIRST FRAME — NOT A SLIDESHOW OF POSES. THIS IS THE SECOND THING THAT WENT WRONG BEFORE.
The animation is ALREADY RUNNING AT FULL SPEED IN FRAME ONE. There is NO still opening frame, NO ease-in, NO ramp-up, NO slow start, NO settling, NO warm-up, NO micro-wobble while it gets going. Santa is mid-bounce at 0.0s and he is still bouncing in the last frame.
Santa moves the way a hand-drawn cartoon moves: every frame is an IN-BETWEEN of the frame before it. His hips, his knees, his shoulders and the bobble of his hat TRAVEL THROUGH each movement along a smooth arc — they are never teleported from one position to another, never cut, never snapped to a different unrelated drawing. There is no jump between unrelated pictures, no reset to the opening pose, no frozen hold, no pause, no settle and no loop restart at any point.
THE RHYTHM IS THE KEY: he is dancing to a beat, so the movement is a CONTINUOUS BOUNCE — down, up, down, up — running unbroken through the whole four seconds at roughly two full bounces per second, with the other business layered on top of that bounce without ever interrupting it. The bounce never stops, not for a single frame.
THE TEST: play any half second of this clip and the motion inside it must flow — his body carries through, his backside travels, the bobble swings on its arc. You must never see the picture blink from one drawing to a different unrelated drawing, and you must never see it hold still.
FLASHING IS NOT MOVEMENT. Colour chases and strobes are allowed but they are secondary — at every instant the thing a viewer sees is a BODY PHYSICALLY TRAVELLING, not lights blinking over a static shape.

THE DANCE, LAYERED ON TOP OF THE UNBROKEN BOUNCE:
0.0-0.5s — already mid-bounce and dropping lower with every beat: his backside pops right out and his knees drive wide, the two motion arcs pulsing in time, while the goblin's fingers spread slowly apart over his eyes.
0.5-1.0s — he starts swinging his hips side to side as he bounces, tracing a slow figure of eight, the black belt whipping across behind him and the white bobble swinging in a long arc over first one shoulder then the other.
1.0-1.5s — he takes one hand off his knee, sweeps it round in a wide slow arc and slaps his own backside on the beat, then brings it back — the arm travelling the whole way, never cutting.
1.5-2.0s — he walks it down: knees dipping alternately, hips sinking lower and lower on each bounce until he is squatting as low as he can go, the whole figure squashing short and wide at the bottom of the dip, while the goblin turns slowly away and hunches his shoulders up around his ears.
2.0-2.5s — he rises smoothly back up, still bouncing, and shimmies his shoulders left-right-left with both hands back on his knees, his beard swinging under his chin.
2.5-3.0s — he twists slowly a quarter turn so one shoulder comes round toward us, one boot lifting and pointing, then twists smoothly back square, the goblin peeking back through his fingers.
3.0-3.5s — he crosses one boot behind the other and grinds down low on the balls of his feet in one continuous sinking turn, the beard swinging out wide, the goblin's striped scarf whipping across him.
3.5-4.0s — he bounces backwards two steps with his backside leading and both hands paddling low at his sides, then drops into the deepest bounce of the whole clip, the goblin throwing both stubby arms out sideways in surrender — and the bounce is still going in the very last frame.

Every change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off across the grid — chunky and stepped, like an animated light-bulb sign — but the movement it describes is continuous and flowing, never a jump between unrelated drawings.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit gable wall above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa and the goblin are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark gable wall between the highest lit point of the artwork — the bobble of Santa's hat — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No boot, no belt, no bobble, no hand, no motion arc and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the roof, the cream trim, the window frames, the sky, the verge, the parked car or the neighbouring houses. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS AND DOWNWARDS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, grinding and swinging — never stretching upward. THE ARMS ARE NEVER RAISED OVERHEAD — Santa's hands stay on or below his knees and the goblin's raised hand stays below the top of his own head. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. (The wall does shift slowly across the frame because the car is driving past — that is the camera, not the artwork.)

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the brown shingled gable behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly wider than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular, never a square pixel matrix. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords — blown-out white core, coloured halo, individually countable. The unlit area is NOT a solid coloured field: it is simply dark gable wall with dull grey unlit bulbs and faint dark cords. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: real sound from inside a slowly rolling car at night — low engine hum, tyres hissing on wet tarmac, faint cold wind through the open window, a distant electrical hum from the display. NO music, NO score, NO background song, NO narration, NO voice-over, nobody speaks in this shot.

LOOK: real phone-camera night footage shot from inside a moving car, natural night exposure, mild sensor noise and grain, faint window reflections, no cinematic bokeh, no colour grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlays, no captions, no watermark, no logos.
